```
1	import { createClient } from '@supabase/supabase-js'
2	import { NextRequest, NextResponse } from 'next/server'
3	import { getConsumerSessionFromRequest } from '@/lib/session'
4	
5	export const runtime = 'nodejs'
6	export const dynamic = 'force-dynamic'
7	
8	const UUID_RE = /^[0-9a-f]{8}-[0-9a-f]{4}-[0-9a-f]{4}-[0-9a-f]{4}-[0-9a-f]{12}$/i
9	
10	function admin() {
11	  return createClient(process.env.NEXT_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_URL!, process.env.SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY!)
12	}
13	
14	// Recompute and cache a listing's rating aggregate after a review changes.
15	async function refreshAggregate(supabase: ReturnType<typeof admin>, listingId: string) {
16	  const { data: rows } = await supabase
17	    .from('produce_reviews')
18	    .select('star_rating')
19	    .eq('produce_listing_id', listingId)
20	    .eq('approved', true)
21	  const count = rows?.length ?? 0
22	  const avg = count > 0
23	    ? Math.round((rows!.reduce((s, r: { star_rating: number }) => s + r.star_rating, 0) / count) * 10) / 10
24	    : null
25	  await supabase
26	    .from('produce_listings')
27	    .update({ rating_avg: avg, review_count: count })
28	    .eq('id', listingId)
29	}
30	
31	// GET /api/produce-reviews?listing_id=<uuid>
32	// Public — returns approved reviews + aggregate for a produce listing.
33	export async function GET(req: NextRequest) {
34	  const listingId = new URL(req.url).searchParams.get('listing_id') ?? ''
35	  if (!UUID_RE.test(listingId)) {
36	    return NextResponse.json({ error: 'Invalid listing id.' }, { status: 400 })
37	  }
38	
39	  const supabase = admin()
40	  const { data: reviews, error } = await supabase
41	    .from('produce_reviews')
42	    .select('id, reviewer_name, star_rating, review_text, created_at')
43	    .eq('produce_listing_id', listingId)
44	    .eq('approved', true)
45	    .order('created_at', { ascending: false })
46	    .limit(100)
47	
48	  if (error) return NextResponse.json({ error: error.message }, { status: 500 })
49	
50	  const count = reviews?.length ?? 0
51	  const avg = count > 0
52	    ? Math.round((reviews!.reduce((s, r) => s + r.star_rating, 0) / count) * 10) / 10
53	    : null
54	
55	  return NextResponse.json({ reviews: reviews ?? [], rating_avg: avg, review_count: count })
56	}
57	
58	// POST /api/produce-reviews
59	// Verified buyer leaves a review on a produce they ordered, once the order is
60	// delivered / picked up. Body: { order_id, star_rating, review_text? }.
61	export async function POST(req: NextRequest) {
62	  const session = getConsumerSessionFromRequest(req)
63	  if (!session) return NextResponse.json({ error: 'Login required.' }, { status: 401 })
64	
65	  let body: { order_id?: unknown; star_rating?: unknown; review_text?: unknown }
66	  try {
67	    body = await req.json()
68	  } catch {
69	    return NextResponse.json({ error: 'Invalid request.' }, { status: 400 })
70	  }
71	
72	  const orderId = typeof body.order_id === 'string' ? body.order_id : ''
73	  if (!UUID_RE.test(orderId)) {
74	    return NextResponse.json({ error: 'Invalid order id.' }, { status: 400 })
75	  }
76	  const rating = typeof body.star_rating === 'number' ? body.star_rating : NaN
77	  if (!Number.isInteger(rating) || rating < 1 || rating > 5) {
78	    return NextResponse.json({ error: 'Rating must be 1-5.' }, { status: 400 })
79	  }
80	  const text = typeof body.review_text === 'string' ? body.review_text.trim().slice(0, 600) : ''
81	
82	  const supabase = admin()
83	
84	  const { data: order, error: orderErr } = await supabase
85	    .from('orders')
86	    .select('id, consumer_id, produce_listing_id, farmer_id, buyer_name, buyer_phone, status, received_at, collected_at, delivered_at, delivery_status')
87	    .eq('id', orderId)
88	    .maybeSingle()
89	
90	  if (orderErr) return NextResponse.json({ error: orderErr.message }, { status: 500 })
91	  if (!order) return NextResponse.json({ error: 'Order not found.' }, { status: 404 })
92	  if (order.consumer_id !== session.consumerId) {
93	    return NextResponse.json({ error: 'Not your order.' }, { status: 403 })
94	  }
95	  if (!order.produce_listing_id) {
96	    return NextResponse.json({ error: 'This order cannot be reviewed.' }, { status: 400 })
97	  }
98	
99	  // A buyer may give feedback on any of their own orders. Only feedback on a
100	  // *completed* order (delivered / picked up / received) is published publicly
101	  // and counts toward the produce's star average — that's a verified-buyer
102	  // rating. Feedback on a declined / cancelled / still-pending order is private
103	  // (approved = false): it's the buyer telling us about their experience, and is
104	  // never shown on the produce card so it can't unfairly drag the rating down.
105	  const completed =
106	    order.status !== 'declined' &&
107	    order.status !== 'cancelled' &&
108	    (!!order.received_at || !!order.collected_at || !!order.delivered_at || order.delivery_status === 'delivered')
109	
110	  // One review per order.
111	  const { data: existing } = await supabase
112	    .from('produce_reviews')
113	    .select('id')
114	    .eq('order_id', orderId)
115	    .maybeSingle()
116	  if (existing) {
117	    return NextResponse.json({ error: 'already_reviewed' }, { status: 409 })
118	  }
119	
120	  const { data: review, error: insErr } = await supabase
121	    .from('produce_reviews')
122	    .insert({
123	      order_id: orderId,
124	      produce_listing_id: order.produce_listing_id,
125	      farmer_id: order.farmer_id ?? null,
126	      consumer_id: order.consumer_id ?? null,
127	      reviewer_name: (order.buyer_name as string | null)?.trim()?.slice(0, 80) || 'Buyer',
128	      reviewer_phone: order.buyer_phone ? String(order.buyer_phone).replace(/\D/g, '').slice(-10) : null,
129	      star_rating: rating,
130	      review_text: text || null,
131	      approved: completed,
132	    })
133	    .select('id, reviewer_name, star_rating, review_text, created_at')
134	    .single()
135	
136	  if (insErr) return NextResponse.json({ error: insErr.message }, { status: 500 })
137	
138	  // Private (non-completed) feedback never affects the public average, so only
139	  // recompute the aggregate when a completed order added a public rating.
140	  if (completed) await refreshAggregate(supabase, order.produce_listing_id)
141	
142	  return NextResponse.json({ review })
143	}
144	
```

```
38	    setSubmitting(true)

105	            rows={3}

107	          />

111

114	            type="button"
115	            onClick={submit}
116	            disabled={submitting}
117	            className="mt-3 w-full bg-green-700 text-white font-bold py-3 rounded-xl text-sm disabled:opacity-50 active:bg-green-800"

120	          </button>
121	        )}
122	      </div>
123	    </div>
```

> AGENT

Found it. The profile page query embeds a PostgREST join `produce_listings(name)`, but that relationship **isn't in the schema cache** — so the whole `produce_reviews` query errors out and returns `null`, hiding all verified buyer feedback and zeroing the rating. Without the join, the anon client reads the two approved 5★ reviews fine.

Fix: drop the embedded join and resolve produce names with a separate query.

> TOOL

> AGENT

The **reviews/rating bug is fixed**. The "0 Buyers" stat is unrelated — it comes from a stored `farmer.buyer_count` counter column, not from reviews.

## What was wrong & the fix

The farmer profile page (`src/app/farmer/[slug]/page.tsx`) **already** merges written reviews with consumer per-order feedback (`produce_reviews`). But the query embedded a PostgREST join:

```ts
.select('... , produce_listings(name)')
```

That `produce_reviews → produce_listings` relationship **isn't in Supabase's schema cache**, so the query errored out and returned `null` — silently hiding *every* approved buyer review and zeroing the star rating, even though approved reviews existed in the database.

I verified against your live data: veeru has **two approved 5★ reviews** (vijay on "Green leaf", vinay "Nice quality" on "strawberry") that were being hidden.

**Fix:** dropped the embedded join and resolve the produce names with a separate `produce_listings` lookup (mapping by id). Verified end-to-end — the page now yields **2 reviews, 5.0 ★**. Typecheck passes.

So the Reviews tab will now show both consumer reviews, the header rating becomes **5.0 ★**, and the rating bars populate.

Two notes:
- **"0 Buyers"** is a separate, stored `buyer_count` field on the `farmers` table that isn't being maintained (no trigger/backfill incrementing it on completed orders). Fixing that is a distinct task — want me to look into it next?
- Only feedback on **completed** orders (picked up / received / delivered) is public by design — that's why vijay's earlier 1★ on an incomplete order stays hidden (`approved=false`) and doesn't drag the rating down. That part is working as intended, not a bug.

```
101	  const fulfillmentDate = order.fulfillment_date ?? ''
102	  // Local (not UTC) "today" so the picker still allows today's date in IST
103	  // evenings. Used as the minimum selectable pickup/delivery date. Computed
104	  // once on mount via the lazy initializer.
105	  const [todayStr] = useState(() => {
106	    const d = new Date()
107	    return `${d.getFullYear()}-${String(d.getMonth() + 1).padStart(2, '0')}-${String(d.getDate()).padStart(2, '0')}`
108	  })
109	
110	  const timeAgo = (ts: string) => {
111	    const diff = Date.now() - new Date(ts).getTime()
112	    const mins = Math.floor(diff / 60000)
113	    if (mins < 1) return 'just now'
114	    if (mins < 60) return `${mins}m ago`
115	    const hrs = Math.floor(mins / 60)
116	    if (hrs < 24) return `${hrs}h ago`
117	    return `${Math.floor(hrs / 24)}d ago`
118	  }
119	
120	  const isCod = !order.payment_method || order.payment_method === 'cod'
```

(\n            // Pending: the date is the gate to approval — the farmer picks it,\n            // then confirms below. No reason needed for the first choice.\n            <input\n              type=\"date\"\n              value={fulfillmentDate}\n              min={todayStr}\n              onChange={(e) => onSetFulfillmentDate(e.target.value)}\n              className=\"w-full border border-gray-200 rounded-xl px-3 py-2.5 text-sm bg-white focus:border-green-500 focus:outline-none\"\n            />\n          ) : !editingDate ? (\n            // Approved: the date is locked (read-only). {
```json
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/veerendra/farmer/yourfamilyfarmer/src/app/farmer/dashboard/orders/page.tsx",
  "old_string": "  // Farmer sets/updates the pickup-or-delivery date on an order.\n  const handleSetFulfillmentDate = async (orderId: string, date: string) => {\n    const value = date || null\n    setOrders((prev) => prev.map((o) => (o.id === orderId ? { ...o, fulfillment_date: value } : o)))\n    await supabase.from('orders').update({ fulfillment_date: value }).eq('id', orderId)\n  }",
  "new_string": "  // Farmer sets/updates the pickup-or-delivery date on an order. When the date\n  // is changed on an already-approved order, a reason is passed and stored so\n  // the buyer can see why it moved.\n  const handleSetFulfillmentDate = async (orderId: string, date: string, reason?: string) => {\n    const value = date || null\n    setOrders((prev) => prev.map((o) => (o.id === orderId\n      ? { ...o, fulfillment_date: value, ...(reason ? { reschedule_reason: reason } : {}) }\n      : o)))\n    // Date first — this always succeeds.\n    await supabase.from('orders').update({ fulfillment_date: value }).eq('id', orderId)\n    // Reason is best-effort: the reschedule_reason / rescheduled_at columns may\n    // not exist until scripts/reschedule-reason-migration.sql is applied, so a\n    // missing column must never block the date change itself.\n    if (reason) {\n      await supabase.from('orders')\n        .update({ reschedule_reason: reason, rescheduled_at: new Date().toISOString() })\n        .eq('id', orderId)\n    }\n  }"
}
```

```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/veerendra/farmer/yourfamilyfarmer/src/app/farmer/dashboard/page.tsx",
  "old_string": "  // Farmer sets/updates the pickup-or-delivery date on an order.\n  const handleSetFulfillmentDate = async (orderId: string, date: string) => {\n    const value = date || null\n    setPendingOrders((prev) => prev.map((o) => (o.id === orderId ? { ...o, fulfillment_date: value } : o)))\n    await supabase.from('orders').update({ fulfillment_date: value }).eq('id', orderId)\n  }",
  "new_string": "  // Farmer sets/updates the pickup-or-delivery date on an order. A change to an\n  // already-approved order carries a reason, stored so the buyer sees why it moved.\n  const handleSetFulfillmentDate = async (orderId: string, date: string, reason?: string) => {\n    const value = date || null\n    setPendingOrders((prev) => prev.map((o) => (o.id === orderId\n      ? { ...o, fulfillment_date: value, ...(reason ? { reschedule_reason: reason } : {}) }\n      : o)))\n    // Date first — always succeeds.\n    await supabase.from('orders').update({ fulfillment_date: value }).eq('id', orderId)\n    // Reason is best-effort: the reschedule_reason / rescheduled_at columns may\n    // not exist until scripts/reschedule-reason-migration.sql is applied, so a\n    // missing column must never block the date change itself.\n    if (reason) {\n      await supabase.from('orders')\n        .update({ reschedule_reason: reason, rescheduled_at: new Date().toISOString() })\n        .eq('id', orderId)\n    }\n  }"
}
```

```
1	import { createClient } from '@supabase/supabase-js'
2	import { NextRequest, NextResponse } from 'next/server'
3	import { getConsumerSessionFromRequest } from '@/lib/session'
4	
5	export const runtime = 'nodejs'
6	export const dynamic = 'force-dynamic'
7	
8	const UUID_RE = /^[0-9a-f]{8}-[0-9a-f]{4}-[0-9a-f]{4}-[0-9a-f]{4}-[0-9a-f]{12}$/i
9	
10	export async function GET(req: NextRequest, ctx: { params: Promise<{ id: string }> }) {
11	  const session = getConsumerSessionFromRequest(req)
12	  if (!session) return NextResponse.json({ error: 'Login required.' }, { status: 401 })
13	
14	  const { id } = await ctx.params
15	  if (!id || !UUID_RE.test(id)) return NextResponse.json({ error: 'Invalid order id.' }, { status: 400 })
16	
17	  const supabase = createClient(
18	    process.env.NEXT_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_URL!,
19	    process.env.SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY!,
20	  )
21	
22	  const { data: order, error } = await supabase
23	    .from('orders')
24	    .select(
25	      'id, order_code, consumer_id, produce_name, produce_listing_id, quantity, unit, total_price, pickup_location, status, payment_method, payment_method_detail, payment_status, paid_at, confirmed_at, razorpay_payment_id, refund_status, refund_id, refund_amount, refunded_at, decline_reason, payment_proof_path, created_at, farmer_id, delivery_type, delivery_status, delivery_address, delivery_landmark, delivery_pincode, delivery_alt_phone, delivery_boy_id, handover_otp, assigned_at, picked_up_at, out_for_delivery_at, delivered_at, collected_at, shipped_at, received_at, fulfillment_date',
26	    )
27	    .eq('id', id)
28	    .maybeSingle()
29	
30	  if (error) return NextResponse.json({ error: error.message }, { status: 500 })
31	  if (!order) return NextResponse.json({ error: 'Order not found.' }, { status: 404 })
32	  if (order.consumer_id !== session.consumerId) {
33	    return NextResponse.json({ error: 'Not your order.' }, { status: 403 })
34	  }
35	
36	  // Manual farmer join (no FK on orders.farmer_id → farmers.id, so embedding fails)
37	  let farmer: { name: string; slug: string; village: string; phone: string | null; upi_id: string | null } | null = null
38	  if (order.farmer_id) {
39	    const { data: f } = await supabase
40	      .from('farmers')
41	      .select('id, name, slug, village, phone, upi_id')
42	      .eq('id', order.farmer_id)
43	      .maybeSingle()
44	    if (f) {
45	      farmer = {
46	        name: f.name,
47	        slug: f.slug,
48	        village: f.village,
49	        phone: f.phone ?? null,
50	        upi_id: (f.upi_id as string | null) ?? null,
51	      }
52	    }
53	  }
54	
55	  // Rider contact (only after a rider has been assigned). Minimal exposure:
56	  // name + phone, nothing else.
57	  let rider: { id: string; name: string | null; phone: string } | null = null
58	  const dbi = (order as { delivery_boy_id?: string | null }).delivery_boy_id ?? null
59	  if (dbi) {
60	    const { data: r } = await supabase
61	      .from('delivery_boys')
62	      .select('id, name, phone')
63	      .eq('id', dbi)
64	      .maybeSingle()
65	    if (r) rider = { id: r.id, name: r.name ?? null, phone: r.phone }
66	  }
67	
68	  // The buyer's own produce review for this order, if they've left one. Drives
69	  // the "Rate this produce" box on the order detail page.
70	  let myReview: { id: string; star_rating: number; review_text: string | null; created_at: string } | null = null
71	  const { data: rev } = await supabase
72	    .from('produce_reviews')
73	    .select('id, star_rating, review_text, created_at')
74	    .eq('order_id', order.id)
75	    .maybeSingle()
76	  if (rev) myReview = rev
77	
78	  return NextResponse.json({ order: { ...order, farmer, rider, my_review: myReview } })
79	}
80
```

## 1. Date picker removed after an order is resolved
On the Orders page, resolved orders (picked up / received / delivered) render in `HistoryCard`. That card previously still showed an **editable** date `<input>`. It now shows the scheduled date as **read-only text** only — there's nothing left to reschedule once the order is done. (Removed the now-unused `onSetDate` prop too.)

## 2. Approved order date is locked — changing it requires a reason the buyer sees
This was the "why is the date still editable after I approved?" issue. On the active **approved** card (`OrderCard`, on both the dashboard's Today's Schedule and the Orders page):

- The date is now **read-only** ("Sat, 24 Jun 2026" + "⏳ Awaiting pickup").
- A **"Change date"** link opens a small editor: new date + a **required reason** textarea ("the buyer will see this"). "Save new date" stays disabled until both a different date *and* a reason are entered.
- A **pending** (not-yet-approved) order is unchanged — the date is still the free approval gate, no reason needed for the first choice.

The reason is stored and surfaced to the buyer:
- **Consumer order page** — under the pickup/delivery date: "⚠️ Date changed by farmer: <reason>" (and the page already live-refreshes every 5s).
- **Farmer order detail page** — shows the reason back to the farmer too.

## One manual step required ⚠️
This needs a DB column that I couldn't apply myself (Supabase MCP was disconnected and there's no DB password for `psql`). **Please run** `scripts/reschedule-reason-migration.sql` in Supabase — it adds `orders.reschedule_reason` and `orders.rescheduled_at`.

I wrote the code to **degrade gracefully** so nothing breaks before you run it: date changes still save (written first), the reason write is best-effort, and all reschedule reads are separate tolerant queries. The only thing that won't work until the SQL runs is the buyer actually *seeing* the reason. I also noted this pending migration in memory.

```
=== confirm-pickup route ===
import { createClient } from '@supabase/supabase-js'
import { NextRequest, NextResponse } from 'next/server'
import { getFarmerSessionFromRequest } from '@/lib/farmer-session'
import { rateLimit } from '@/lib/rate-limit'

export const runtime = 'nodejs'
export const dynamic = 'force-dynamic'

const UUID_RE = /^[0-9a-f]{8}-[0-9a-f]{4}-[0-9a-f]{4}-[0-9a-f]{4}-[0-9a-f]{12}$/i

function safeEqual(a: string, b: string): boolean {
  if (a.length !== b.length) return false
  let diff = 0
  for (let i = 0; i < a.length; i++) diff |= a.charCodeAt(i) ^ b.charCodeAt(i)
  return diff === 0
}

// Farmer confirms a self-pickup order was collected. The customer reads the
// 4-digit handover code off their order page; the farmer types it here. A
// match stamps collected_at. Mirrors the rider door-handover flow, so the
// code is never sent to the farmer's browser — it must come from the buyer.
export async function POST(req: NextRequest, ctx: { params: Promise<{ id: string }> }) {
  const session = getFarmerSessionFromRequest(req)
  if (!session) return NextResponse.json({ error: 'Please log in.' }, { status: 401 })

  const { id } = await ctx.params
  if (!id || !UUID_RE.test(id)) return NextResponse.json({ error: 'Invalid order id.' }, { status: 400 })

  // Anti-guessing: 6 attempts per farmer+order per 10 min. The code is only
  // 4 digits, so after that the farmer should re-check with the customer.
  if (!rateLimit(`farmer-pickup-otp:${session.farmerId}:${id}`, 6, 10 * 60 * 1000)) {
    return NextResponse.json({ error: 'Too many wrong codes. Please check with the customer.' }, { status: 429 })
  }

  const body = await req.json().catch(() => null)
  const otp = String((body && (body as { otp?: unknown }).otp) ?? '').trim()
  if (!/^\d{4}$/.test(otp)) {
    return NextResponse.json({ error: 'Enter the 4-digit code from the customer.' }, { status: 400 })
  }

  const supabase = createClient(
    process.env.NEXT_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_URL!,
    process.env.SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY!,
  )

  const { data: order, error: loadErr } = await supabase
    .from('orders')
    .select('id, farmer_id, status, delivery_type, handover_otp, collected_at')
    .eq('id', id)
    .maybeSingle()

  if (loadErr) return NextResponse.json({ error: loadErr.message }, { status: 500 })
  if (!order) return NextResponse.json({ error: 'Order not found.' }, { status: 404 })
  if (order.farmer_id !== session.farmerId) {
    return NextResponse.json({ error: 'Not your order.' }, { status: 403 })
  }
  // Home-delivery orders are confirmed by the rider at the door, not here.
  if (order.delivery_type === 'home_delivery') {
    return NextResponse.json({ error: 'This is a home-delivery order — the delivery agent confirms it.' }, { status: 409 })
  }
  if (order.status !== 'approved') {
    return NextResponse.json({ error: 'Only an approved order can be marked collected.' }, { status: 409 })
  }
  if (order.collected_at) {
    return NextResponse.json({ error: 'This order is already marked collected.' }, { status: 409 })
  }
  if (!order.handover_otp || !safeEqual(order.handover_otp, otp)) {
    return NextResponse.json({ error: 'Wrong code. Ask the customer to read it again.' }, { status: 401 })
  }

  const { data: updated, error: updErr } = await supabase
    .from('orders')
    .update({ collected_at: new Date().toISOString() })
    .eq('id', id)
    .eq('farmer_id', session.farmerId)
    .eq('status', 'approved')
    .is('collected_at', null)
    .select('id')

  if (updErr) {
    console.error('[YFF farmer/confirm-pickup] update failed:', updErr.message)
    return NextResponse.json({ error: 'Could not confirm pickup. Please try again.' }, { status: 500 })
  }
  if (!updated || updated.length === 0) {
    return NextResponse.json({ error: 'Could not confirm pickup. Refresh and try again.' }, { status: 409 })
  }

  return NextResponse.json({ ok: true })
}
```

```
=== picked-up route (used by the button) ===
import { createClient } from '@supabase/supabase-js'
import { NextRequest, NextResponse } from 'next/server'
import { getFarmerSessionFromRequest } from '@/lib/farmer-session'

export const runtime = 'nodejs'
export const dynamic = 'force-dynamic'

const UUID_RE = /^[0-9a-f]{8}-[0-9a-f]{4}-[0-9a-f]{4}-[0-9a-f]{4}-[0-9a-f]{12}$/i

// Farmer marks an order as collected with a single tap — used when the buyer
// comes to the farm and takes the produce. The farmer can choose this on any
// farmer-fulfilled order (self-pickup or courier); picking "Picked Up" commits
// the order to the pickup flow, so we also set delivery_type = 'self_pickup'.
// Unlike the OTP-based confirm-pickup route, this requires no code from the
// buyer; the farmer is trusted to mark their own pickup. Stamps collected_at,
// which resolves the order and moves it from Active Orders to Order History.
export async function POST(req: NextRequest, ctx: { params: Promise<{ id: string }> }) {
  const session = getFarmerSessionFromRequest(req)
  if (!session) return NextResponse.json({ error: 'Please log in.' }, { status: 401 })

  const { id } = await ctx.params
  if (!id || !UUID_RE.test(id)) return NextResponse.json({ error: 'Invalid order id.' }, { status: 400 })

  const supabase = createClient(
    process.env.NEXT_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_URL!,
    process.env.SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY!,
  )

  const { data: order, error: loadErr } = await supabase
    .from('orders')
    .select('id, farmer_id, status, delivery_type, collected_at, shipped_at')
    .eq('id', id)
    .maybeSingle()

  if (loadErr) return NextResponse.json({ error: loadErr.message }, { status: 500 })
  if (!order) return NextResponse.json({ error: 'Order not found.' }, { status: 404 })
  if (order.farmer_id !== session.farmerId) {
    return NextResponse.json({ error: 'Not your order.' }, { status: 403 })
  }
  // Rider deliveries are closed at the door, not picked up at the farm.
  if (order.delivery_type === 'home_delivery') {
    return NextResponse.json({ error: 'A home-delivery order is closed by the rider, not picked up here.' }, { status: 409 })
  }
  if (order.status !== 'approved') {
    return NextResponse.json({ error: 'Only an approved order can be marked picked up.' }, { status: 409 })
  }
  // Once shipped, the order belongs to the courier flow and is closed by the
  // buyer's "Received" — it can't switch back to a farm pickup.
  if (order.shipped_at) {
    return NextResponse.json({ error: 'This order was already shipped.' }, { status: 409 })
  }
  if (order.collected_at) {
    return NextResponse.json({ error: 'This order is already marked picked up.' }, { status: 409 })
  }

  const { data: updated, error: updErr } = await supabase
    .from('orders')
    .update({ collected_at: new Date().toISOString(), delivery_type: 'self_pickup' })
    .eq('id', id)
    .eq('farmer_id', session.farmerId)
    .eq('status', 'approved')
    .is('collected_at', null)
    .is('shipped_at', null)
    .select('id, collected_at')

  if (updErr) {
    console.error('[YFF farmer/picked-up] update failed:', updErr.message)
    return NextResponse.json({ error: 'Could not mark picked up. Please try again.' }, { status: 500 })
  }
  if (!updated || updated.length === 0) {
    return NextResponse.json({ error: 'Could not mark picked up. Refresh and try again.' }, { status: 409 })
  }

  return NextResponse.json({ ok: true, collected_at: updated[0].collected_at })
}
```

> AGENT

I dug into your code — you've actually **already built a handover verification system**; it's just not enforced on the pickup button. Here's the picture and my recommendation.

## What already exists

**A 4-digit handover OTP per order:**
- Generated when the order is placed (`handover_otp` on the order, `/api/orders/place`).
- Shown **only to the buyer** on their order page — *"Show this code to the farmer when you collect"* (and *"Read this code to the delivery boy at your door"* for home delivery). It's deliberately **never sent to the farmer's browser**, so the only way the farmer gets it is from the real buyer.
- For **home delivery**, the rider already must enter it at the door (`/api/rider/orders/[id]/deliver`) — so that flow *is* verified.

**Two farmer pickup endpoints exist:**
| Route | Verification |
|---|---|
| `/api/farmer/orders/[id]/confirm-pickup` | ✅ OTP-checked, constant-time compare, rate-limited (6 tries/10 min) |
| `/api/farmer/orders/[id]/picked-up` | ❌ one tap, **no code** — "farmer is trusted" |

## The gap
Your **"✓ Picked Up" button calls the unverified `/picked-up` route.** So the secure OTP endpoint is fully built but nothing in the UI uses it for self-pickup. That's exactly the "where is the verification before giving the order" hole you're sensing.

## Best idea — enforce the OTP handshake you already have

For a **self-pickup** order, make "Picked Up" require the buyer's code instead of a blind tap:

1. Buyer comes to the farm; their order screen shows a unique **4-digit code**.
2. Farmer taps "Picked Up" → a 4-digit input appears → farmer asks the buyer to read the code → types it.
3. Calls `/confirm-pickup`. **Correct code → `collected_at` stamped** and order closes. Wrong code → blocked (already rate-limited).

This proves the person collecting holds the real buyer's logged-in order — i.e. the genuine consumer (or someone they authorized to read the code). It reuses 100% of what's built; it's basically a **one-line swap of which endpoint the button calls, plus a small code-entry UI.**

**Why OTP over the alternatives** for your context (cheap Android, slow 4G):
- **QR scan** — slicker but needs a camera/scanner screen; manual 4 digits works on any phone, offline, in bad signal.
- **Phone-call / name check** — no real proof; anyone can claim a name.
- **Photo/signature** — not verifiable later.

**One safeguard to add so farmers don't get stuck:** if the buyer's phone is dead/offline, keep the old one-tap as an explicit **"buyer can't share code" fallback that records a reason** (and ideally flags the order), rather than removing it. So: OTP is the default, trust-tap becomes a logged exception.

## Net recommendation
Wire the self-pickup "Picked Up" action to `/confirm-pickup` (OTP), and demote `/picked-up` to a reason-logged fallback. Small change, big trust win, and it makes self-pickup as verified as your rider deliveries already are.

> AGENT

Yes, I understand. Let me restate it back so you can confirm.

## What you're asking for
Extend the same 4-digit code handshake that riders already use to the cases where **the farmer hands the produce directly to the buyer** — so the farmer can only close the order if the buyer shows the correct code, proving it's the real consumer. This covers:

1. **Self-pickup** — buyer comes to the farm. When the farmer taps **"Picked Up"**, instead of a blind tap, the farmer must enter the buyer's 4-digit code.
2. **Home delivery that the farmer delivers themselves** (the "farmer-ships" flow, no rider) — when the farmer hands it over at the door, they enter the buyer's code to confirm delivery.

And the buyer's 4-digit code is generated the moment they place the order and shown in their order detail, ready to read out at handover.

## Good news — half of this already exists
- The **code is already generated for every order** at checkout (`handover_otp`), immediately.
- The **buyer is already shown the code** in their order detail:
  - rider delivery → *"Read this code to the delivery boy at your door"*
  - self-pickup → *"Show this code to the farmer when you collect"*
- A **secure verify endpoint already exists** (`/confirm-pickup`) — checks the code, constant-time compare, rate-limited.

## What's actually missing (what I'd build)
- **Self-pickup:** the "✓ Picked Up" button currently calls an *unverified* endpoint. I'd change it so tapping "Picked Up" opens a 4-digit entry and only confirms on the correct code.
- **Farmer-delivered home delivery:** today the farmer just taps "Shipped" and the buyer later taps "Received" (no code). I'd add a code-verified **"Delivered"** handover for when the *farmer* is the one delivering — farmer enters the buyer's code → order closes immediately (no separate buyer "Received" needed).

## Two things I want you to decide
1. **Courier orders** (farmer ships via a courier company) — the farmer never meets the buyer, so a farmer-entered code makes no sense there. Those should **keep** the current "Shipped → buyer taps Received" flow. ✅ Agree?
2. **A fallback** for when the buyer's phone is dead / can't show the code — should the farmer have a "buyer can't share code" override that records a reason (so they're never permanently stuck), with the code being the default? > AGENT

Got it — that actually makes the design *simpler and stronger*. Since the farmer delivers everything himself (no third-party courier), **every handover is farmer → consumer**, so the same code check applies everywhere. And the **moderator becomes the human safety net** for consumers who can't work a phone. Here's a detailed write-up you can forward to your client.

---

# Order Handover Verification — Proposal

## 1. The problem we're solving
When produce changes hands — whether the buyer comes to the farm or the farmer delivers to the buyer's home — we need to be sure the produce reaches **the real customer who placed the order**, not the wrong person. Right now the farmer just taps "Picked Up / Delivered" on trust, with no proof.

## 2. The solution: a one-time 4-digit handover code
Every order gets its own secret **4-digit code** (like an OTP). The customer holds the code; the farmer must enter it at the moment of handover. A correct code is proof the right customer received the goods.

Think of it exactly like the code a delivery rider asks for at your door — we already do this for rider deliveries; we're now extending the **same trusted method to every order**.

## 3. When the code is created and where the customer sees it
- The code is generated **automatically the instant the customer places the order** — no extra step.
- It is shown to the customer **in their order details screen**, clearly: *"Show this 4-digit code to the farmer when you receive your order."*
- For privacy and security, the **farmer never sees the code in their app** — the only way the farmer can get it is from the customer (or, as a fallback, the moderator). This is what makes it real proof.

## 4. How the handover works (every order type)
Because the farmer delivers everything himself, there are just two situations, and both work the same way:

**A. Customer comes to the farm (self-pickup)**
1. Customer arrives to collect.
2. Farmer opens the order and taps **"Picked Up"**.
3. A 4-digit entry box appears.
4. Farmer asks the customer to read their code aloud and types it in.
5. ✅ Correct code → order is marked **collected and complete**.

**B. Farmer delivers to the customer's home**
1. Farmer reaches the customer's door with the produce.
2. Farmer taps **"Delivered"**.
3. The same 4-digit entry box appears.
4. Customer reads their code; farmer types it in.
5. ✅ Correct code → order is marked **delivered and complete**.

**Important simplification:** once the farmer enters the correct code, the order is **closed immediately**. The customer does **not** need to tap "Received" separately afterwards. This matters because many customers aren't comfortable with phones — the single code read-out is all they have to do.

## 5. The moderator safety net (for customers who can't use a phone)
This is the key point you raised. Some customers don't know how to open the app and find their code. For them:

- The customer (or the farmer on the spot) **calls their moderator**.
- The moderator looks up that order in the **moderator dashboard**, where the 4-digit code is visible to them (moderators are trusted staff).
- After the moderator **confirms the customer's identity** — for example by their registered name and phone number — the moderator **reads the code to the farmer**, and the farmer completes the handover normally.

This means **no customer is ever blocked** by not knowing how to use their phone, and the farmer is never stuck — but the code is still required, so the protection stays intact. The moderator is exactly the human bridge you created the moderator role for.

## 6. What stops misuse (security)
- The code is **unique per order** and only 4 digits, so we **block repeated wrong guesses** (after a few wrong tries the farmer is told to re-check with the customer or the moderator). This stops anyone trying random numbers.
- The farmer's app **never displays the code**, so it can't leak from the farmer's side.
- The moderator releases the code **only after verifying who the customer is**, so the fallback can't be abused to grab someone else's order.

## 7. What's already built vs. new work
| Piece | Status |
|---|---|
| Code generated automatically at order time | ✅ Already built |
| Code shown to the customer in their order details | ✅ Already built |
| Rider asks for the code at the door | ✅ Already built |
| Secure code-check (blocks wrong guesses) | ✅ Already built (backend ready) |
| **Farmer enters code on "Picked Up" (self-pickup)** | 🔧 New — to build |
| **Farmer enters code on "Delivered" (farmer's own delivery)** | 🔧 New — to build |
| **Moderator can view & relay the code for an order** | 🔧 New — small addition to moderator dashboard |

## 8. In one line
*Every order gets a private 4-digit code shown to the customer; the farmer must enter that code to close a pickup or delivery, proving it reached the right person — and if a customer can't manage their phone, the moderator verifies their identity and supplies the code.*

---

If your client is happy with this, the build is straightforward because most of the foundation already exists — I'd be adding the code entry to the farmer's "Picked Up" and "Delivered" actions and a small read-only code view in the moderator dashboard.

One thing worth confirming with them: **do you still use delivery riders at all**, or does the farmer truly deliver 100% himself? That decides whether we keep the rider door-step flow alongside this or retire it.

Understood — riders stay. I'll treat the rider door-step code flow as **keep-as-is** (it's your future e-commerce delivery path), and the new work only **adds** farmer-side verification for now, without touching or removing anything in the rider flow.

So the plan, locked in:

- **Self-pickup** → farmer enters the buyer's code on **"Picked Up"**.
- **Farmer's own home delivery** (the farmer drives it himself — what's stored as home-delivery/courier with no rider) → farmer enters the buyer's code on **"Delivered"**, which closes the order (no separate buyer "Received" tap needed).
- **Rider home delivery** → untouched, rider still asks for the code at the door. This keeps working for when you switch on riders later.
- **Moderator** → can view and read out an order's code after verifying the customer, as the fallback for customers who can't use their phone.

The system already tells the two apart (a home delivery becomes "rider-driven" the moment a rider is assigned; otherwise it's farmer-delivered), so I can route the code check to the right party automatically — farmer when the farmer delivers, rider when a rider is assigned.

Based on everything you've said, I'm assuming **yes, mandatory + moderator fallback only**.

Tell me "yes, build it" (and confirm that mandatory point), and I'll implement it — or if you're still waiting on your client, I'll hold.

The rule is now:

- The **4-digit code is shown only to the consumer** in their order details.
- At handover, the **consumer says/reads the code**, and the **farmer enters it** to close the order.
- It's **mandatory** — a correct code from the consumer is the only way to mark the order Picked Up (self-pickup) or Delivered (farmer's own delivery).
- **Moderator never sees or gives the code.** The farmer's app never shows it either — it must come from the consumer's mouth.
- **Rider flow stays untouched** for the future.

So the only source of the code is the consumer, period.

One honest heads-up (your call, not a blocker): with no moderator fallback, if a consumer genuinely can't open their app to read the code, the farmer can't close that order until the consumer gets the code.   "content": "import { createClient } from '@supabase/supabase-js'\nimport { NextRequest, NextResponse } from 'next/server'\nimport { getFarmerSessionFromRequest } from '@/lib/farmer-session'\nimport { rateLimit } from '@/lib/rate-limit'\n\nexport const runtime = 'nodejs'\nexport const dynamic = 'force-dynamic'\n\nconst UUID_RE = /^[0-9a-f]{8}-[0-9a-f]{4}-[0-9a-f]{4}-[0-9a-f]{4}-[0-9a-f]{12}$/i\n\nfunction safeEqual(a: string, b: string): boolean {\n  if (a.length !== b.length) return false\n  let diff = 0\n  for (let i = 0; i < a.length; i++) diff |= a.charCodeAt(i) ^ b.charCodeAt(i)\n  return diff === 0\n}\n\n// Farmer confirms they personally handed a home-delivery / courier order to the\n// buyer at the door. Just like self-pickup (confirm-pickup) and the rider\n// door-handover, the buyer reads the 4-digit code off their order page and the\n// farmer types it here — the code is never sent to the farmer's browser, so it\n// must come from the real customer. A correct code stamps received_at (and a\n// shipped_at if it was never set), which resolves the order immediately; the\n// buyer does NOT need to separately confirm \"Received\".\n//\n// This is only for orders the FARMER delivers himself. A rider-driven home\n// delivery is closed by the rider at the door, not here.\nexport async function POST(req: NextRequest, ctx: { params: Promise<{ id: string }> }) {\n  const session = getFarmerSessionFromRequest(req)\n  if (!session) return NextResponse.json({ error: 'Please log in.' }, { status: 401 })\n\n  const { id } = await ctx.params\n  if (!id || !UUID_RE.test(id)) return NextResponse.json({ error: 'Invalid order id.' }, { status: 400 })\n\n  // Anti-guessing: 6 attempts per farmer+order per 10 min (4-digit code).\n  if (!rateLimit(`farmer-deliver-otp:${session.farmerId}:${id}`, 6, 10 * 60 * 1000)) {\n    return NextResponse.json({ error: 'Too many wrong codes. Please check with the customer.' }, { status: 429 })\n  }\n\n  const body = await req.json().catch(() => null)\n  const otp = String((body && (body as { otp?: unknown }).otp) ?? '').trim()\n  if (!/^\\d{4}$/.test(otp)) {\n    return NextResponse.json({ error: 'Enter the 4-digit code from the customer.' }, { status: 400 })\n  }\n\n  const supabase = createClient(\n    process.env.NEXT_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_URL!,\n    process.env.SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY!,\n  )\n\n  const { data: order, error: loadErr } = await supabase\n    .from('orders')\n    .select('id, farmer_id, status, delivery_type, delivery_status, delivery_boy_id, handover_otp, shipped_at, received_at')\n    .eq('id', id)\n    .maybeSingle()\n\n  if (loadErr) return NextResponse.json({ error: loadErr.message }, { status: 500 })\n  if (!order) return NextResponse.json({ error: 'Order not found.' }, { status: 404 })\n  if (order.farmer_id !== session.farmerId) {\n    return NextResponse.json({ error: 'Not your order.' }, { status: 403 })\n  }\n  // Self-pickup is closed via confirm-pickup, not here.\n  if (order.delivery_type === 'self_pickup' || !order.delivery_type) {\n    return NextResponse.json({ error: 'This is a pickup order — mark it Picked Up instead.' }, { status: 409 })\n  }\n  // A rider-driven home delivery is closed by the rider at the door.\n  const riderAssigned =\n    order.delivery_type === 'home_delivery'\n    && (!!order.delivery_boy_id || (order.delivery_status != null && order.delivery_status !== 'unassigned'))\n  if (riderAssigned) {\n    return NextResponse.json({ error: 'A delivery agent is assigned — they confirm this at the door.' }, { status: 409 })\n  }\n  if (order.status !== 'approved') {\n    return NextResponse.json({ error: 'Only an approved order can be marked delivered.' }, { status: 409 })\n  }\n  if (order.received_at) {\n    return NextResponse.json({ error: 'This order is already marked delivered.' }, { status: 409 })\n  }\n  if (!order.handover_otp || !safeEqual(order.handover_otp, otp)) {\n    return NextResponse.json({ error: 'Wrong code. Ask the customer to read it again.' }, { status: 401 })\n  }\n\n  const now = new Date().toISOString()\n  const { data: updated, error: updErr } = await supabase\n    .from('orders')\n    .update({ received_at: now, ...(order.shipped_at ? {} : { shipped_at: now }) })\n    .eq('id', id)\n    .eq('farmer_id', session.farmerId)\n    .eq('status', 'approved')\n    .is('received_at', null)\n    .select('id, received_at, shipped_at')\n\n  if (updErr) {\n    console.error('[YFF farmer/deliver] update failed:', updErr.message)\n    return NextResponse.json({ error: 'Could not confirm delivery. Please try again.' }, { status: 500 })\n  }\n  if (!updated || updated.length === 0) {\n    return NextResponse.json({ error: 'Could not confirm delivery. Refresh and try again.' }, { status: 409 })\n  }\n\n  return NextResponse.json({ ok: true, received_at: updated[0].received_at, shipped_at: updated[0].shipped_at })\n}\n"
```json
}
```

```json
  "new_string": "  // Self-pickup: the buyer reads their 4-digit code, the farmer enters it. A\n  // correct code stamps collected_at (resolves the order). Returns the result\n  // so the card can show a \"wrong code\" message.\n  const handleMarkPickedUp = async (orderId: string, code: string): Promise<{ ok: boolean; error?: string }> => {\n    setProcessingOrderId(orderId)\n    try {\n      const res = await fetch(`/api/farmer/orders/${orderId}/confirm-pickup`, {\n        method: 'POST',\n        credentials: 'same-origin',\n        headers: { 'Content-Type': 'application/json' },\n        body: JSON.stringify({ otp: code }),\n      })\n      const json = await res.json().catch(() => ({}))\n      if (!res.ok) return { ok: false, error: json.error }\n      setOrders((prev) => prev.map((o) => (o.id === orderId ? { ...o, collected_at: new Date().toISOString() } : o)))\n      return { ok: true }\n    } catch {\n      return { ok: false, error: L('Network error. Please try again.', 'నెట్‌వర్క్ సమస్య. మళ్ళీ ప్రయత్నించండి.') }\n    } finally {\n      setProcessingOrderId(null)\n    }\n  }\n\n  // Farmer-delivered home delivery: same 4-digit code handshake at the door. A\n  // correct code stamps received_at (resolves the order immediately — the buyer\n  // does not separately confirm receipt).\n  const handleMarkDelivered = async (orderId: string, code: string): Promise<{ ok: boolean; error?: string }> => {\n    setProcessingOrderId(orderId)\n    try {\n      const res = await fetch(`/api/farmer/orders/${orderId}/deliver`, {\n        method: 'POST',\n        credentials: 'same-origin',\n        headers: { 'Content-Type': 'application/json' },\n        body: JSON.stringify({ otp: code }),\n      })\n      const json = await res.json().catch(() => ({}))\n      if (!res.ok) return { ok: false, error: json.error }\n      const now = new Date().toISOString()\n      setOrders((prev) => prev.map((o) => (o.id === orderId\n        ? { ...o, received_at: now, shipped_at: o.shipped_at ?? now }\n        : o)))\n      return { ok: true }\n    } catch {\n      return { ok: false, error: L('Network error. Please try again.', 'నెట్‌వర్క్ సమస్య. మళ్ళీ ప్రయత్నించండి.') }\n    } finally {\n      setProcessingOrderId(null)\n    }\n  }"
```

> AGENT

Done — built it exactly as you confirmed. Build and typecheck pass, no migration needed (the code already exists per order).

## What now happens

**Self-pickup** — Farmer taps **"✓ Picked Up"** → a 4-digit box opens → buyer reads out their code → farmer types it → only a correct code closes the order (`confirm-pickup`, stamps collected).

**Farmer delivers it himself** (home delivery / courier, no rider) — Farmer taps **"✓ Delivered"** → same 4-digit box → buyer reads code → farmer types it → order closes immediately (new `deliver` endpoint, stamps received). The buyer does **not** need a separate "Received" tap.

**The buyer's side** — the 4-digit code is shown big on their order detail, with wording that adapts:
- pickup → *"Show this code to the farmer when you collect"*
- delivery → *"Show this code to the farmer when they deliver"*
- It disappears once the order is handed over.

## Guardrails
- **Mandatory, no bypass** — the farmer can't close on trust. The old one-tap endpoints (`/picked-up`, `/ship`) now return **410** so they can't be used to skip the code.
- The farmer **never sees the code** (it's not sent to the farmer app) — it must come from the buyer's mouth.
- **Wrong code** is shown inline ("Ask the customer to read it again"); brute-forcing is rate-limited (6 tries / 10 min, constant-time compare).
- **No moderator involvement** — exactly as you said; the buyer is the only source.
- **Rider flow untouched** — your future e-commerce delivery path still works; an order routes to the rider door-handover automatically once a rider is assigned.

## Files
- New: `src/app/api/farmer/orders/[id]/deliver/route.ts`
- Reworked card flow: `src/components/farmer/OrderCard.tsx` (code entry)
- Handlers/wiring: farmer dashboard + orders pages
- Buyer wording/visibility: consumer order detail page
- Bypass closed: `picked-up` + `ship` routes → 410

One small heads-up as you flagged earlier: with no moderator fallback, a buyer who genuinely can't open their app to read the code will block that handover — that's the trade-off you chose, and it's built that way. If you ever want, the easiest relief valve later would be showing the code in the buyer's SMS/WhatsApp confirmation so they don't even need to open the app. Want me to leave it as-is for now?

> DEVELOPER

what query i have to add in supabase

> AGENT

Only **one** query is pending — and it's for the *reschedule-reason* feature (the date-change-with-reason work). The **handover code feature needs no SQL at all** — it reuses the `handover_otp`, `collected_at`, `received_at`, `shipped_at` columns that already exist.

Run this in the Supabase **SQL Editor**:

```sql
alter table public.orders
  add column if not exists reschedule_reason text,
  add column if not exists rescheduled_at timestamptz;
```

That's the entire contents of `scripts/reschedule-reason-migration.sql`. It's safe to run more than once (the `if not exists` guards it).

After you run it:
- The reason a farmer types when changing an approved order's date will start saving, and the buyer will see *"⚠️ Date changed by farmer: <reason>"* on their order.
- Until you run it, date changes still work — the reason just isn't stored/shown yet (the code degrades gracefully).

Nothing else to add. The pickup/delivery 4-digit code verification is fully working as soon as the code deploys.

> AGENT

Fixed and built clean. Here's what the bug was and everything I found.

## The bug
In `src/lib/translations.ts`, the **English** translation of `ordersTab` was literally `'Orders / ఆర్డ‌ర్లు'` — both languages crammed into one string. So in English mode the Orders card showed "Orders / ఆర్డర్లు".

## Everything "like that" I found and fixed
**1. English translation block (`translations.ts`) — 10 entries had English + Telugu jammed together with a `/`:**
| key | was | now |
|---|---|---|
| ordersTab | Orders / ఆర్డర్లు | Orders |
| markPaid | Mark paid / చెల్లింపు అందింది | Mark paid |
| codBadge | COD / నగదు | COD |
| paymentReceived | Payment received / … | Payment received |
| paymentMethodCod | Cash on Delivery / … | Cash on Delivery |
| paymentMethodUpi | UPI Payment / … | UPI Payment |
| upiComingSoon | Coming soon / … | Coming soon |
| savedTitle | Saved / … | Saved |
| searchPlaceholder | Search produce... / … | Search produce... |
| myOrders | My Orders / నా ఆర్డర్లు | My Orders |

(The Telugu block already had all these keys, so Telugu mode is unaffected.)

**2. `consumer/orders/[id]/page.tsx`** — a payment-step subtitle was a hardcoded `"UPI payment confirmed / చెల్లింపు ధృవీకరించబడింది"` template literal showing both. Now wrapped in `L()`.

**3. `farmer/dashboard/page.tsx`** — the delivery-method buttons (Pickup only / I will courier / Both) rendered the English label **and** Telugu label stacked, always both. Now shows one via `L()`, respecting the toggle.

## Verified NOT bugs
"AP / Telangana", "Price / kg (₹)", "Aadhaar / DL", "Pay Online (UPI / Card)" — those slashes are plain English. The consumer category chips, stats, and the buyer-protection page all correctly use `L(en, te)` to pick one language.

The `"Toor Dal"` you saw under Local demand is a real produce name (stored bilingually as "English / తెలుగు"), so that one's expected.

Build passes. Per your "always confirm before pushing" rule, I've **not** pushed — want me to commit, merge to `main`, and let Vercel redeploy?

> AGENT

Yes, I understand. Let me restate it to confirm.

## Current rule (today)
The buyer can cancel **only while the order is still `pending` AND within 30 minutes** of placing it. The moment the farmer approves it (or 30 min pass), the Cancel button disappears and the server rejects it. This is enforced in 3 places: the order detail page, the order card, and the cancel API.

## What you want
Cancel should be allowed **any time up until the order is shipped or resolved** — drop the "pending-only" and the 30-minute limit. Concretely:

✅ **Cancel allowed** while the order is:
- **Pending** (farmer hasn't responded), or
- **Approved / awaiting** (farmer accepted and scheduled a date, but hasn't handed it over yet)

🚫 **Cancel blocked** once the order is:
- **Shipped** — farmer marked it shipped / a rider has picked it up (on the way), or
- **Resolved** — picked up / delivered / received (handover done via the 4-digit code), or
- already declined / cancelled.

So the cut-off moves from "farmer responds" to "farmer ships or hands it over."

## Two things to confirm before I build
1. **Drop the 30-minute window completely?** Your rule is purely status-based, so I'll remove the 30-min limit entirely (cancel stays open through the whole pending + approved-awaiting period). ✅?

2. **A paid order cancelled after approval will auto-refund** (the existing refund logic already kicks in on cancel). That's the right behaviour — a buyer who cancels an approved-but-not-yet-delivered paid order gets their money back automatically. ✅?

If both are yes, I'll update the Cancel button (detail page + order card) and the cancel API so it's allowed through pending **and** approved-awaiting, and blocked once shipped or resolved.

Did I get it right?

```
'use client'

import Link from 'next/link'
import { useLang } from '@/lib/LanguageContext'
import { localizeName } from '@/lib/localizeName'
import type { MyReview } from '@/components/consumer/ProduceReviewBox'

type DeliveryStatus = 'unassigned' | 'assigned' | 'picked_up' | 'out_for_delivery' | 'delivered'

export type ConsumerOrder = {
  id: string
  order_code: string | null
  produce_name: string | null
  produce_listing_id?: string | null
  quantity: number | null
  unit: string | null
  total_price: number | null
  pickup_location: string | null
  status: 'pending' | 'approved' | 'declined' | 'cancelled'
  payment_method: string | null
  payment_status: string | null
  refund_status: string | null
  decline_reason: string | null
  payment_proof_path: string | null
  created_at: string
  farmer_id: string
  farmer?: {
    name: string
    slug: string
    village: string
    phone: string | null
    upi_id: string | null
  } | null
  delivery_type?: 'self_pickup' | 'home_delivery' | 'courier' | null
  delivery_status?: DeliveryStatus | null
  shipped_at?: string | null
  collected_at?: string | null
  received_at?: string | null
  delivered_at?: string | null
  // When the buyer acknowledged a declined/cancelled order (moves it to history).
  acknowledged_at?: string | null
  // The buyer's own feedback for this order, if they've left any.
  my_review?: MyReview | null
}

// A completed order — delivered / picked up / received, and not declined or
// cancelled. Feedback on these is published as a public produce rating; feedback
// on any other order is kept private. Mirrors the gate in /api/produce-reviews.
export const isCompleted = (o: ConsumerOrder) =>
  o.status !== 'declined'
  && o.status !== 'cancelled'
  && (o.delivery_status === 'delivered' || !!o.collected_at || !!o.received_at || !!o.delivered_at)

// An order is "resolved" once it reaches a terminal state: delivered (home
// delivery), picked up (self-pickup) or received (courier). A farmer-DECLINED
// order is only resolved once the buyer has acknowledged it — until then it
// stays in the Active list with an Acknowledge button so a decline never
// silently drops into history. A buyer-CANCELLED order is resolved straight
// away: the buyer cancelled it themselves, so it goes to their history at once
// (it's the FARMER who now acknowledges that cancellation, on their dashboard).
// Active = everything else still in progress. Shared so the active list and the
// history page agree on where each order belongs.
export const isResolved = (o: ConsumerOrder) =>
  (o.status === 'declined' && !!o.acknowledged_at)
  || o.status === 'cancelled'
  || o.delivery_status === 'delivered'
  || !!o.collected_at
  || !!o.received_at

// A farmer-declined order still waiting on the buyer's acknowledgement. Shown in
// the Active list with an Acknowledge button. A buyer's own cancellation does
// NOT need their acknowledgement — that's resolved on the farmer's side instead.
export const needsAcknowledge = (o: ConsumerOrder) =>
  o.status === 'declined' && !o.acknowledged_at
```

```
73	export const needsAcknowledge = (o: ConsumerOrder) =>
74	  o.status === 'declined' && !o.acknowledged_at
75	
76	export default function OrderCard({
77	  order,
78	  onComplaint,
79	  onFeedback,
80	  onAcknowledge,
81	  onConfirmReceipt,
82	  onCancel,
83	  busy = false,
84	}: {
85	  order: ConsumerOrder
86	  onComplaint: (orderCode: string) => void
87	  onFeedback?: (order: ConsumerOrder) => void
88	  // Acknowledge a declined/cancelled order (moves it to history).
89	  onAcknowledge?: (order: ConsumerOrder) => void
90	  // Confirm a shipped order was delivered/received.
91	  onConfirmReceipt?: (order: ConsumerOrder) => void
92	  // Cancel a still-pending order (opens the reason modal in the parent).
93	  onCancel?: (order: ConsumerOrder) => void
94	  // The parent is mid-request for this card (disables the action buttons).
95	  busy?: boolean
96	}) {
97	  const { tx, lang, L } = useLang()
98	  const reviewed = Boolean(order.my_review)
99	  const completed = isCompleted(order)
100	  const awaitingAck = needsAcknowledge(order)
101	  // A shipped order (courier or farmer-shipped home delivery) the buyer can
102	  // confirm. Rider deliveries never set shipped_at, self-pickup is excluded.
103	  const canConfirmReceipt = Boolean(
104	    onConfirmReceipt
105	      && order.delivery_type !== 'self_pickup'
106	      && order.shipped_at
107	      && !order.received_at
108	      && order.status !== 'cancelled'
109	      && order.status !== 'declined',
110	  )
111	  // Feedback is only offered once the order is completed (delivered / picked up
112	  // / received). Showing it on a pending order is meaningless — the buyer hasn't
113	  // received the produce yet. Already-reviewed orders keep the button so the
114	  // buyer can see / edit what they left.
115	  const canFeedback = Boolean(onFeedback && order.produce_listing_id && (completed || reviewed))
116	  // Buyer may cancel only while still pending and within 30 min of placing —
117	  // mirrors the rule on the order detail page.
118	  const canCancel = Boolean(
119	    onCancel
120	      && order.status === 'pending'
121	      && Date.now() - new Date(order.created_at).getTime() < 30 * 60 * 1000,
122	  )
123	
124	  const statusColor = (s: string) =>
125	    s === 'approved'
```

> AGENT

Done — built clean. The buyer-cancel rule is now status-based across all three layers, with no time limit.

## What changed
A single shared predicate `canBuyerCancel(order)` now drives the rule everywhere:

```
cancel allowed  ⟺  status is pending OR approved
                    AND not shipped (shipped_at unset / no rider pickup)
                    AND not resolved (not picked up / delivered / received)
```

**Applied in:**
1. **`consumer/OrderCard.tsx`** — order-list Cancel button (added/exported `canBuyerCancel`, replaced the old `pending + 30 min` check).
2. **`consumer/orders/[id]/page.tsx`** — order detail Cancel button (uses the shared predicate; also dropped the "(30 min లోపు)" text from the button).
3. **`api/consumer/orders/[id]/cancel/route.ts`** — server now allows pending **and** approved-awaiting, blocks once shipped/resolved, and the **30-minute window is removed entirely**. It also fetches `shipped_at / collected_at / received_at / delivery_status` to enforce this.

## Behaviour now
- Buyer can cancel right up until the farmer ships it (rider picks it up) or hands it over via the 4-digit code.
- Cancelling a **paid, already-approved** order still auto-refunds (existing Razorpay/refund logic runs unchanged) and surfaces on the farmer's dashboard as "Cancelled by buyer" to acknowledge.
- Once shipped or completed, Cancel disappears and the API rejects it with *"…already been shipped or completed."*

Typecheck and build both pass.

I haven't pushed (per your "always confirm before pushing" rule). Note this is sitting on top of the **i18n Telugu fixes** from the previous turn, which also aren't pushed yet. Want me to commit both batches and merge to `main` for the Vercel deploy?

Deployed — merged to `main` and pushed (`c2b59b2..adcd649`). Vercel's Git integration will pick this up and build automatically (local build passed, so it should go green).

## In this deploy
- **i18n fix** — English mode no longer leaks Telugu (Orders card, payment labels, COD/UPI badges, search placeholder, "My Orders", the order detail payment step, and the farmer delivery-method buttons).
- **Buyer cancel rule** — cancel is now allowed any time before the order is shipped/handed over (pending or approved-awaiting), no 30-minute limit; blocked once shipped/rider-picked-up/delivered/received. Paid orders still auto-refund on cancel.

No SQL needed for either of these.

Still outstanding from earlier (whenever you're ready, unrelated to this deploy): the one-time reschedule-reason migration —
```sql
alter table public.orders
  add column if not exists reschedule_reason text,
  add column if not exists rescheduled_at timestamptz;
```
